\[

\]

   - Point 1 (\(x_1, y_1\)) = \( (5, 0) \)
   - Point 2 (\(x_2, y_2\)) = \( (9, 0) \)

   \[

The problem involves finding the slope (m₂) of a second line given that it forms an angle θ = π/4 with another line having a known slope m₁ = 1/4.

We use the formula for the tangent of the angle between two lines:
\[
\tan \theta = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2} \right|
\]
Given that θ = π/4, we know tan(π/4) = 1. Substituting the known values:
\[
1 = \left| \frac{m_2 - \frac{1}{4}}{1 + \frac{1}{4} m_2} \right|
\]
This results in two equations due to the absolute value:
1. \( \frac{m_2 - \frac{1}{4}}{1 + \frac{1}{4} m_2} = 1 \)
2. \( \frac{m_2 - \frac{1}{4}}{1 + \frac{1}{4} m_2} = -1 \)

Solving the first equation:
\[
m_2 - \frac{1}{4} = 1 + \frac{1}{4} m_2 \\
0.75m_2 = 1.25 \\
m_2 = \frac{5}{3}
\]

Solving the second equation:
\[
m_2 - \frac{1}{4} = -1 - \frac{1}{4} m_2 \\
1.25m_2 = -0.75 \\
m_2 = -\frac{3}{5}
\]

Thus, the possible slopes are \( \boxed{\frac{5}{3}} \) and \( \boxed{-\frac{3}{5}} \).

We are given two points and need to find the value of \( x \) such that the line passing through \((10, 14)\) and \((x, 26)\) has the same slope as the line passing through \((0, 8)\) and \((6, 10)\).

**Step 1: Calculate the Slope of the First Line**

The formula for the slope \( m \) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Applying this to the points \((0, 8)\) and \((6, 10)\):
\[
m = \frac{10 - 8}{6 - 0} = \frac{2}{6} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

**Step 2: Set Up the Equation for the Second Line**

Since both lines are parallel, they have the same slope. Therefore, the slope between \((10, 14)\) and \((x, 26)\) is also \( \frac{1}{3} \).

Using the slope formula:
\[
\frac{26 - 14}{x - 10} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

**Step 3: Solve for \( x \)**

Simplify the equation:
\[
\frac{12}{x - 10} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

Cross-multiply to solve for \( x \):
\[
12 \times 3 = x - 10
\]
\[
36 = x - 10
\]
\[
x = 36 + 10
\]
\[
x = 46
\]

The value of \( x \) is **46**.

To determine the slope between two given points on the Cartesian plane and calculate the area under the curve formed by connecting these four points:

**Given Points:**
- Point A: (-6, 7)
- Point B: (2, 9)
- Point C: (7, -3)
- Point D: (-2, 0)

### Step-by-Step Calculations:

#### 1. Calculating the Slope Between Two Points:
Let's calculate the slope between points A and B.

**Formula for Slope (m):**
\[ m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} \]

**Applying the Values:**
- \( y_2 = 9 \) (Point B)
- \( y_1 = 7 \) (Point A)
- \( x_2 = 2 \) (Point B)
- \( x_1 = -6 \) (Point A)

\[ m = \frac{9 - 7}{2 - (-6)} = \frac{2}{8} = 0.25 \]

**Slope between Points A and B:** 0.25

#### 2. Calculating the Area Under the Curve:
To calculate the area under the curve formed by connecting these four points, we can use the **Shoelace Formula**, which is suitable for polygons when their vertices are known.

**Formula for Shoelace Method:**
\[ \text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} |(x_1y_2 + x_2y_3 + x_3y_4 + x_4y_1) - (y_1x_2 + y_2x_3 + y_3x_4 + y_4x_1)| \]

**Applying the Values:**
- \( x_1 = -6, y_1 = 7 \)
- \( x_2 = 2, y_2 = 9 \)
- \( x_3 = 7, y_3 = -3 \)
- \( x_4 = -2, y_4 = 0 \)

**Calculating the First Part:**
\[ (-6)(9) + (2)(-3) + (7)(0) + (-2)(7) = -54 - 6 + 0 - 14 = -74 \]

**Calculating the Second Part:**
\[ (7)(2) + (9)(7) + (-3)(-2) + (0)(-6) = 14 + 63 + 6 + 0 = 83 \]

**Subtracting and Taking Absolute Value:**
\[ |-74 - 83| = | -157 | = 157 \]

**Calculating the Area:**
\[ \text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times 157 = 78.5 \, \text{square units} \]

### Final Answer:

    "Area Under the Curve": 78.5
  }
}

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Determine the Angle with Respect to the X-Axis:**
   - The given angle is 60° with respect to the y-axis.
   - Since the total angle around a point is 90°, the angle with respect to the x-axis is:
     \[
     90^\circ - 60^\circ = 30^\circ
     \]

2. **Calculate the Slope Using Tangent:**
   - The slope \( m \) of a line is given by the tangent of its angle \( θ \) with respect to the x-axis:
     \[
     m = \tan(θ)
     \]
   - Substituting \( θ = 30^\circ \):
     \[
     m = \tan(30^\circ) = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}
     \]

3. **Simplify the Slope:**
   - The slope can also be expressed as:
     \[
     m = \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
     \]
   - However, it's often preferable to rationalize the denominator, resulting in:
     \[
     m = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}
     \]

**Final Answer:**
The slope of the line is \( \boxed{\sqrt{3}} \).

Slope 2 = (3 - 0)/(3 - 4) = 3/-1 = -3

For the fourth opposite side:
Slope 4 = (-1 - 2)/(-2 - (-3)) = -3/1 = -3

Since both pairs of opposite sides have equal slopes, the figure is a parallelogram. To check if it's a rectangle (i.e., has right angles), I'll look at the product of the slopes.

Product of Slope 1 and Slope 2:
(1/6) * (-3) = -0.5

Since the product is not equal to -1, the sides are not perpendicular, so it's not a rectangle.
</think>

To determine whether the points \((-2, -1)\), \((4, 0)\), \((3, 3)\), and \((-3, 2)\) form a parallelogram with right angles (i.e., a rectangle), we can use slopes to check for both parallelism and perpendicularity.

### Step-by-Step Explanation:

1. **Check for Parallel Sides:**

   - **Slope between \((-2, -1)\) and \((4, 0)\):**
     \[
     m_1 = \frac{0 - (-1)}{4 - (-2)} = \frac{1}{6}
     \]
   
   - **Slope between \((3, 3)\) and \((-3, 2)\):**
     \[
     m_2 = \frac{2 - 3}{-3 - 3} = \frac{-1}{-6} = \frac{1}{6}
     \]
   
   Since \(m_1 = m_2\), these sides are parallel.

   - **Slope between \((4, 0)\) and \((3, 3)\):**
     \[
     m_3 = \frac{3 - 0}{3 - 4} = \frac{3}{-1} = -3
     \]
   
   - **Slope between \((-3, 2)\) and \((-2, -1)\):**
     \[
     m_4 = \frac{-1 - 2}{-2 - (-3)} = \frac{-3}{1} = -3
     \]
   
   Since \(m_3 = m_4\), these sides are also parallel.

2. **Check for Perpendicular Sides:**

   For the figure to have right angles, adjacent sides must be perpendicular (i.e., their slopes' product equals \(-1\)).

   - **Product of \(m_1\) and \(m_3\):**
     \[
     m_1 \times m_3 = \frac{1}{6} \times (-3) = -\frac{1}{2}
     \]
   
   Since the product is not equal to \(-1\), the sides are not perpendicular.

### Conclusion:

The points form a parallelogram but do not have right angles. Therefore, it **is not** a rectangle.

{"Final Answer": "It is not a rectangle."}

The problem involves finding two lines where one slope (m₁) is twice another (m₂), i.e., m₁ = 2m₂. The tangent of the angle between them is given as tanθ = 1/3.

Using the formula:
\[ \tan\theta = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2} \right| \]

Substituting \( m₁ = 2m₂ \):
\[ \tan\theta = \left| \frac{m₂ - 2m₂}{1 + 2m₂^2} \right| = \left| \frac{-m₂}{1 + 2m₂^2} \right| = \frac{|m₂|}{1 + 2m₂^2} \]

Given \( \tan\theta = \frac{1}{3} \):
\[ \frac{|m₂|}{1 + 2m₂^2} = \frac{1}{3} \]
Solving for m₂, we get:
Case 1: \( m₂ = \frac{1}{2} \) (positive slope)
Case 2: \( m₂ = -\frac{1}{2} \) (negative slope)

Therefore, the slopes are:

- For positive case:
  - \( m₁ = 1 \), \( m₂ = \frac{1}{2} \)
- For negative case:
  - \( m₁ = -1 \), \( m₂ = -\frac{1}{2} \)

**Solution:**

To find the equation of the line passing through the points \((3, 1)\) and \((5, 7)\), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**
   
   The slope \(m\) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   
   Substituting the given points:
   \[
   m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
   \]

2. **Use Point-Slope Form:**
   
   The point-slope form of a line's equation is:
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   
   Using one of the points, say \((3, 1)\):
   \[
   y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
   \]

3. **Simplify to Slope-Intercept Form:**
   
   To get the equation in slope-intercept form (\(y = mx + b\)):
   \[
   y - 1 = 3x - 9
   \]
   \[
   y = 3x - 9 + 1
   \]
   \[
   y = 3x - 8
   \]

**Final Answer:**

The equation of the line is:
\[
\boxed{\text{Final Answer}: "y=3x-8"}
\]

First, I need to find the equation of a line that makes an angle of \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) radians with the positive x-axis and passes through the point (0, 2).

I know that the slope (\( m \)) of a line can be determined using the tangent of the angle it makes with the x-axis. So, \( m = \tan\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right) \). Calculating this gives \( m = -\sqrt{3} \).

With the slope known and the line passing through (0, 2), which is the y-intercept (\( b \)), the equation of the line in slope-intercept form is:
\[ y = mx + b \]
Substituting the values:
\[ y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 2 \]

Next, to find a parallel line, I keep the slope the same but change the y-intercept. Let's choose \( b = 4 \) for simplicity, resulting in:
\[ y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 4 \]

Finally, to determine where this original line intersects the y-axis, I set \( x = 0 \):
\[ y = -\sqrt{3}(0) + 2 \]
Which simplifies to:
\[ y = 2 \]
So, the line intersects the y-axis at (0, 2).
</think>

To find the equation of a line that makes an angle of \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) radians with the positive x-axis and passes through the point \( (0, 2) \), follow these steps:

1. **Determine the Slope (\( m \))**:
   The slope is given by the tangent of the angle:
   \[
   m = \tan\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right)
   \]
   Knowing that \( \tan\left(\frac{\pi}{3}\right) = \sqrt{3} \), and since \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) is in the second quadrant where tangent is negative:
   \[
   m = -\sqrt{3}
   \]

2. **Equation of the Line**:
   Using the slope-intercept form \( y = mx + b \) and knowing that the line passes through \( (0, 2) \), which is the y-intercept (\( b \)):
   \[
   y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 2
   \]

3. **Equation of a Parallel Line**:
   A parallel line will have the same slope but a different y-intercept. Let's choose \( b = 4 \) for simplicity:
   \[
   y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 4
   \]

4. **Y-Intercept of the Original Line**:
   The original line already passes through \( (0, 2) \), so its y-intercept is at \( (0, 2) \).

### Final Answer

The equation of the required lines and their y-intercept are:

\[
\boxed{
  \text{Original Line Equation: } y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 2
}
\]
\[
\boxed{
  \text{Parallel Line Equation: } y = -\sqrt{3}\ x + 4
}
\]
\[
\boxed{
  \text{Y-Intercept of Original Line: } (0, 2)
}
\]

Let's determine the equation of a line that passes through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8).

**Step 1: Calculate the Slope**

The slope (\( m \)) between two points \( (x_1, y_1) \) and \( (x_2, y_2) \) is given by:

\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Substituting the given points:

\[
m = \frac{8 - 7}{-5 - 4} = \frac{1}{-9} = -\frac{1}{9}
\]

**Step 2: Use Point-Slope Form**

The point-slope form of a line's equation is:

\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]

Using the point (4, 7):

\[
y - 7 = -\frac{1}{9}(x - 4)
\]

**Step 3: Convert to Standard Form**

To eliminate the fraction, multiply both sides by 9:

\[
9(y - 7) = -(x - 4)
\]

Expanding and simplifying:

\[
9y - 63 = -x + 4
\]
\[
x + 9y = 67
\]

**Final Answer:**

The equation of the line in standard form is:

\[
\boxed{x + 9y = 67}
\]

To find the **distance** from the point \((5, -7)\) to the line \(5x - 6y - 28 = 0\), we can use the formula for the distance from a point to a line:

\[
d = \frac{|Ax_0 + By_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

**Where:**
- \(A = 5\)
- \(B = -6\)
- \(C = -28\)
- \(x_0 = 5\)
- \(y_0 = -7\)

### Step-by-Step Calculation:

1. **Calculate the numerator:**
   \[
   |5(5) + (-6)(-7) - 28| = |25 + 42 - 28| = |39|
   \]

2. **Calculate the denominator:**
   \[
   \sqrt{5^2 + (-6)^2} = \sqrt{25 + 36} = \sqrt{61}
   \]

3. **Compute the distance:**
   \[
   d = \frac{39}{\sqrt{61}}
   \]
   
4. **Simplify if possible (optional):**
   If desired, rationalize the denominator:
   \[
   d = \frac{39\sqrt{61}}{61}
   \]

### Final Answer:

The distance from the point \((5, -7)\) to the line \(5x - 6y -28 = 0\) is:

\[
\boxed{\frac{39}{\sqrt{61}}}
\]

First, I need to identify the coordinates of the given point and understand the equation of the line. The point is (1,5), and the line is represented by the equation 3x - 4y - 16 = 0.

To find the distance from the point to the line, I'll use the distance formula for a point and a line. This formula calculates the shortest perpendicular distance between the two.

I'll substitute the values of x₁ as 1 and y₁ as 5 into the formula. Next, I'll compute the numerator by plugging these coordinates into the line equation's left-hand side and taking the absolute value.

For the denominator, I'll calculate the square root of the sum of the squares of the coefficients of x and y from the line equation.

After computing both the numerator and the denominator, I'll divide them to find the distance. Finally, I'll present the result as a simplified fraction.
</think>

```json
{
  "Final Answer": "The distance is \( \frac{1}{5} \)."
}
```

Step-by-Step Explanation:

1. **Identify the Given Point and Line Equation:**
   - Point: (1, 5)
   - Line: 3x - 4y - 16 = 0

2. **Use the Distance Formula from a Point to a Line:**
   
   The distance \( d \) from a point \( (x_1, y_1) \) to the line \( ax + by + c = 0 \) is given by:
   
   \[
   d = \frac{|a x_1 + b y_1 + c|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}}
   \]

3. **Plug in the Values:**
   
   - Here, \( a = 3 \), \( b = -4 \), and \( c = -16 \).
   - Substitute these into the formula:
     
     \[
     d = \frac{|3(1) + (-4)(5) + (-16)|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}}
     \]

4. **Calculate Numerator:**
   
   \[
   3(1) - 4(5) - 16 = 3 - 20 - 16 = -33
   \]
   
   Take the absolute value:
   
   \[
   | -33 | = 33
   \]

5. **Calculate Denominator:**
   
   \[
   \sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16} = \sqrt{25} = 5
   \]

6. **Compute the Distance:**
   
   \[
   d = \frac{33}{5} = 6\frac{3}{5}
   \]
   
   However, correcting the earlier step:
   
   Upon re-evaluating:

   The correct numerator after applying the formula is actually:

   \[
   |3(1) - 4(5) - 16| = |3 - 20 - 16| = |-33| = 33
   \]
   
   But, considering the proper application of the line equation:
   
   The correct calculation is:

   \[
   d = \frac{|3(1) - 4(5) - 16|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}} = \frac{3}{5}
   \]

7. **Final Answer:**
   
   \[
   \boxed{\dfrac{3}{5}}
   \]

The given line is \(7x - 9y - 19 = 0\). The slope of this line is found by converting it to slope-intercept form:
\[ y = \frac{7}{9}x - \frac{19}{9} \]
So, the slope (\(m_1\)) is \( \frac{7}{9} \).

For two lines to be perpendicular, the product of their slopes must be \(-1\). Let \(m_2\) be the slope of the line passing through points \((H, 5)\) and \((6, 3)\):
\[ m_1 \times m_2 = -1 \]
\[ \frac{7}{9} \times m_2 = -1 \]
\[ m_2 = -\frac{9}{7} \]

The slope of the line through \((H, 5)\) and \((6, 3)\) is:
\[ m_2 = \frac{3 - 5}{6 - H} = \frac{-2}{6 - H} \]
Set this equal to \( -\frac{9}{7} \):
\[ \frac{-2}{6 - H} = -\frac{9}{7} \]

Cross-multiplying:
\[ (-2) \times 7 = (-9) \times (6 - H) \]
\[ -14 = -54 + 9H \]
\[ 40 = 9H \]
\[ H = \frac{40}{9} \]

**Final Answer:**  
The value of \(H\) is \(\boxed{\dfrac{40}{9}}\).

First, I need to find the angle between the two lines given by the equations √3 x + y = 1 and x + √3 y = 1.

To do this, I'll start by finding the slopes of each line. For the first equation, solving for y gives me y = -√3 x + 1, so the slope (m₁) is -√3.

For the second equation, solving for y yields y = (-1/√3)x + 1/√3, which means the slope (m₂) is -1/√3.

Next, I'll use the formula for the tangent of the angle between two lines:
tan θ = |(m₂ - m₁)/(1 + m₁*m₂)|

Substituting the slopes into the formula:
tan θ = |[(-1/√3) - (-√3)] / [1 + (-√3)*(-1/√3)]|
= |[(-1/√3) + √3] / [1 + 1]|
= |[( (√3)^2 - 1 ) / √3] / 2|
= |( (3 - 1)/√3 ) / 2|
= |(2/√3) / 2|
= |1/√3|

This simplifies to tan θ = 1/√3, which corresponds to an angle of 30 degrees.

Finally, the acute angle between the two lines is 30 degrees.

To find the equation of a line that passes through (5, 0) and is parallel to the given line \(x - 7y + 5 = 0\):

1. **Determine the slope of the original line**:  
   Rewrite the equation in slope-intercept form:  
   \[
   x - 7y + 5 = 0 \implies y = \frac{1}{7}x + \frac{5}{7}
   \]  
   The slope \(m\) is \(\frac{1}{7}\).

2. **Use the point-slope form for the new line**:  
   Since the new line must have the same slope (\(\frac{1}{7}\)) and pass through (5, 0):  
   \[
   y - 0 = \frac{1}{7}(x - 5) \implies y = \frac{1}{7}x - \frac{5}{7}
   \]

3. **Convert to standard form**:  
   Multiply both sides by 7:  
   \[
   7y = x - 5 \implies x - 7y - 5 = 0
   \]

**Final Answer**: The equation of the desired line is \(x - 7y - 5 = 0\).

To find the **equation of a line** that is **parallel** to the given line \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) and passes through the point \((-4, 5)\), follow these steps:

1. **Find the slope of the original line:**

   \[
   5x - 6y + 4 = 0 \\
   -6y = -5x - 4 \\
   y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{2}{3}
   \]
   
   The slope (\(m\)) is \(\frac{5}{6}\).

2. **Use the point-slope form to find the equation of the parallel line:**
   
   Since parallel lines have the same slope, use \(m = \frac{5}{6}\) and the point \((-4, 5)\).
   
   The point-slope formula is:
   
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   
   Plugging in the values:
   
   \[

   \]

To determine the distance between two parallel lines, I first need to confirm that they are indeed parallel.

I can check this by comparing their slopes. The general form of a linear equation is Ax + By + C = 0, and the slope (m) is given by -A/B.

For the first line, 19x + 12y - 38 = 0, the slope is:
m₁ = -19/12

For the second line, 38x + 24y + 70 = 0, the slope is:
m₂ = -38/24, which simplifies to -19/12.

Since m₁ equals m₂, both lines have the same slope and are therefore parallel.

Next, I can use the formula for the distance between two parallel lines. The formula is:

Distance (d) = |C₂ - C₁| / sqrt(A² + B²)

To apply this formula, it's better to express both equations in a similar form where their constants term match one of the coefficients.

Let me multiply the first equation by 2 to make the constant term comparable with the second equation:
38x + 24y - 76 = 0

Now, comparing this with the second equation, which is:
38x + 24y + 70 = 0

Here, A = 38, B = 24, C₁ (from first modified equation) = -76, and C₂ = 70.

Plugging these values into the distance formula:

d = |70 - (-76)| / sqrt(38² + 24²)
= |146| / sqrt(1444 + 576)
= 146 / sqrt(2020)

Simplifying further, I can reduce the fraction:

Divide numerator and denominator by 2:
d = 73 / (sqrt(2020)/2) ≈ 73/4.497

Calculating this gives approximately d ≈ 16.25.

Therefore, the distance between the two parallel lines is approximately 16.25 units.
</think>

To find the **distance** between the two **parallel lines**, we can use the formula for the distance from a point to a line when the lines are expressed in standard form (Ax + By + C = 0).

### Given Lines:
1. \( 19x + 12y - 38 = 0 \)
2. \( 38x + 24y + 70 = 0 \)

### Step-by-Step Solution:

#### 1. Check if the Lines are Parallel
To confirm that the lines are parallel, we can check their slopes.

- **Slope of First Line (m₁):**
  \[
  m₁ = -\frac{A}{B} = -\frac{19}{12}
  \]

- **Slope of Second Line (m₂):**
  \[
  m₂ = -\frac{38}{24} = -\frac{19}{12}
  \]
  
Since \( m₁ = m₂ \), both lines are indeed parallel.

#### 2. Use the Distance Formula
The formula to find the distance (\(d\)) between two parallel lines is:
\[
d = \frac{|C₂ - C₁|}{\sqrt{A² + B²}}
\]

However, for accurate calculation, it's better to express both equations in a comparable form. Let’s adjust the first equation by multiplying it by 2:

- **Adjusted First Equation:**
  \[
  38x + 24y - 76 = 0
  \]
  
Now, we can compare:
- \( A = 38 \)
- \( B = 24 \)
- \( C₁' = -76 \) (from the adjusted first equation)
- \( C₂ = 70 \) (from the second equation)

#### 3. Apply the Distance Formula
\[
d = \frac{|C₂ - C₁'|}{\sqrt{A² + B²}} = \frac{|70 - (-76)|}{\sqrt{38² + 24²}}
\]
\[
d = \frac{|146|}{\sqrt{1444 + 576}} = \frac{146}{\sqrt{2020}}
\]

Simplify the denominator:
\[
\sqrt{2020} ≈ 44.96
\]
\[
d ≈ \frac{146}{44.96} ≈ 3.25
\]

### Final Answer:
The distance between the two parallel lines is approximately **3.25 units**.

To determine the point where the line \(\frac{x}{3} + \frac{y}{4} = 1\) intersects the **x-axis**, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Intersection with the X-Axis
Any point on the x-axis has a \( y = 0 \).

### Step 2: Substitute \( y = 0 \) into the Line Equation
\[
\frac{x}{3} + \frac{0}{4} = 1 \\
\Rightarrow \frac{x}{3} = 1 \\
\Rightarrow x = 3
\]

### Step 3: Identify the Point of Intersection
The point where the line intersects the x-axis is \( (3, 0) \).

### Step 4: Calculate the Distance Between This Point and Itself

To find the **distance** from the point \((1, 2)\) to the line given by the equation \(4x + 7y + 5 = 0\), we can use the formula for the distance from a point to a line.

### Formula:
The distance \(d\) from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is given by:

\[
d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

### Given:
- Point: \((x_0, y_0) = (1, 2)\)
- Line equation: \(4x + 7y + 5 = 0\)  
  Here, \(A = 4\), \(B = 7\), and \(C = 5\)

### Calculation:

1. **Calculate the numerator**:
   \[
   |4(1) + 7(2) + 5| = |4 + 14 + 5| = |23| = 23
   \]

2. **Calculate the denominator**:
   \[
   \sqrt{4^2 + 7^2} = \sqrt{16 + 49} = \sqrt{65}
   \]

3. **Compute the distance**:
   \[
   d = \frac{23}{\sqrt{65}}
   \]

### Final Answer:

The distance from the point (1, 2) to the line \(4x + 7y + 5 = 0\) is:

To determine the value of \( x \) for the points \( (10,\ 14) \) and \( (x,\ 26) \), we need to ensure that these two points lie on a line with the same slope as the line passing through the points \( (0,\ 8) \) and \( (6,\ 10) \).

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope of the First Line
The formula for the slope (\( m \)) between two points \( (x_1, y_1) \) and \( (x_2, y_2) \) is:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Applying this to the points \( (0,\ 8) \) and \( (6,\ 10) \):
\[
m = \frac{10 - 8}{6 - 0} = \frac{2}{6} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

### Step 2: Set Up the Equation for the Second Line
The slope between the points \( (10,\ 14) \) and \( (x,\ 26) \) should be equal to \( \frac{1}{3} \):
\[
\frac{26 - 14}{x - 10} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

### Step 3: Solve for \( x \)
Simplify the equation:
\[
\frac{12}{x - 10} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

Cross-multiply to solve for \( x \):
\[
12 \times 3 = x - 10
\]
\[
36 = x - 10
\]
\[
x = 36 + 10
\]
\[

These two lines form sides at vertex A(-6, 7) in a parallelogram.

**Final Answer:**

- \( y = -\frac{10}{13}x + \frac{31}{13} \)

I need to find a point on the x-axis that is equidistant from the points (9,8) and (5,6). 

First, I'll let the point on the x-axis be represented as (x, 0).

Next, I'll use the distance formula for both distances: 
Distance between (x,0) and (9,8), which is sqrt[(x - 9)^2 + (0 - 8)^2].
Distance between (x,0) and (5,6), which is sqrt[(x - 5)^2 + (0 - 6)^2].

Since the distances are equal, I'll set these two expressions equal to each other:
sqrt[(x - 9)^2 + (-8)^2] = sqrt[(x - 5)^2 + (-6)^2].

To eliminate the square roots, I'll square both sides of the equation:
(x - 9)^2 + 64 = (x - 5)^2 + 36.

Expanding both squares:
(x^2 - 18x + 81) + 64 = (x^2 - 10x + 25) + 36.

Simplifying each side:
x^2 - 18x + 145 = x^2 - 10x + 61.

Subtracting x^2 from both sides:
-18x + 145 = -10x + 61.

Bringing like terms together:
-8x = -84.

Finally, solving for x gives me x = 10.5.

To find a point on the x-axis that is equidistant from the points (9, 8) and (5, 6), let's follow these steps:

1. **Let the Point on the X-Axis Be (x, 0):**
   
2. **Use the Distance Formula for Both Points:**
   - Distance between (x, 0) and (9, 8):
     \[
     \sqrt{(x - 9)^2 + (0 - 8)^2} = \sqrt{(x - 9)^2 + 64}
     \]
   - Distance between (x, 0) and (5, 6):
     \[
     \sqrt{(x - 5)^2 + (0 - 6)^2} = \sqrt{(x - 5)^2 + 36}
     \]

3. **Set the Distances Equal to Each Other:**
   \[
   \sqrt{(x - 9)^2 + 64} = \sqrt{(x - 5)^2 + 36}
   \]

4. **Square Both Sides to Eliminate the Square Roots:**
   \[
   (x - 9)^2 + 64 = (x - 5)^2 + 36
   \]
   
5. **Expand and Simplify:**
   \[
   x^2 - 18x + 81 + 64 = x^2 - 10x + 25 + 36
   \]
   \[
   x^2 - 18x + 145 = x^2 - 10x + 61
   \]

6. **Subtract \(x^2\) from Both Sides:**
   \[
   -18x + 145 = -10x + 61
   \]

7. **Solve for \(x\):**
   \[
   -8x + 145 = 61
   \]
   \[
   -8x = -84
   \]
   \[
   x = \frac{84}{8} = 10.5
   \]

**Final Answer:**

\boxed{\text{The point on the x-axis is } (10.5, 0)}

***********************<think>
To find the slope of the line passing through points P(2, -2) and Q(10, 2), I will use the slope formula.

First, calculate the change in y by subtracting the y-coordinate of point P from that of point Q. This gives me \( 2 - (-2) = 4 \).

Next, calculate the change in x by subtracting the x-coordinate of point P from that of point Q. This results in \( 10 - 2 = 8 \).

Finally, divide the change in y by the change in x to find the slope: \( \frac{4}{8} = 0.5 \).
</think>

I'll start by calculating the slopes of the lines connecting each pair of points. The slope between two points (x₁, y₁) and (x₂, y₂) is given by (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁).

1. **Slope between (4, 4) and (3, 5):**
   Slope = (5 - 4)/(3 - 4) = 1/(-1) = -1

2. **Slope between (3, 5) and (-1, -1):**
   Slope = (-1 - 5)/(-1 - 3) = (-6)/(-4) = 3/2

3. **Slope between (-1, -1) and (4, 4):**
   Slope = (4 - (-1))/(4 - (-1)) = 5/5 = 1

Next, I'll check if any two slopes are negative reciprocals of each other because perpendicular lines have slopes that multiply to -1.

- **Slope of first line:** -1
- **Negative reciprocal of -1:** 1
- The third slope is indeed 1, which matches the negative reciprocal.

Since one pair of slopes is negative reciprocals, this confirms that the corresponding sides are perpendicular, forming a right angle.

To determine if the points \((4, 4)\), \((3, 5)\), and \((-1, -1)\) form a right-angled triangle without using the Pythagorean theorem, we can examine the slopes of the lines connecting these points. If any two sides are perpendicular, their slopes will be negative reciprocals (i.e., their product will be \(-1\)).

**Step 1: Calculate the Slopes**

1. **Slope between \((4, 4)\) and \((3, 5)\):**
   \[
   m_1 = \frac{5 - 4}{3 - 4} = \frac{1}{-1} = -1
   \]

2. **Slope between \((3, 5)\) and \((-1, -1)\):**
   \[
   m_2 = \frac{-1 - 5}{-1 - 3} = \frac{-6}{-4} = \frac{3}{2}
   \]

3. **Slope between \((-1, -1)\) and \((4, 4)\):**
   \[
   m_3 = \frac{4 - (-1)}{4 - (-1)} = \frac{5}{5} = 1
   \]

**Step 2: Check for Perpendicularity**

Two lines are perpendicular if the product of their slopes is \(-1\).

- \(m_1\) and \(m_3\):
  \[
  m_1 \times m_3 = (-1) \times (1) = -1
  \]

Since \(m_1\) and \(m_3\) are negative reciprocals, the lines with these slopes are perpendicular.

   Slope = (0 - (-1)) / (4 - (-2)) = 1/6

2. **Slope between (4, 0) and (3, 3):**
   Slope = (3 - 0)/(3 - 4) = 3/-1 = -3

3. **Slope between (3, 3) and (-3, 2):**

   Slope = (-1 - 2)/(-2 - (-3)) = -3/1 = -3

From these calculations, I observe that the slopes of the sides connecting (-2, -1)-(4,0) and (3,3)-(-3,2) are both 1/6, indicating they are parallel. Similarly, the slopes of the other two sides are both -3, showing they are also parallel.

This confirms that the figure is a trapezoid with both pairs of opposite sides being parallel.

Next, to determine if it's an isosceles trapezoid, I need to check if the non-parallel sides (the legs) are equal in length.

1. **Length between (-2, -1) and (3, 3):**

- The second pair has a slope of -3; its perpendicular would be 1/3. Again, no matching slopes.

To determine if the given points form an isosceles trapezoid that is not rectangular (i.e., with any pair of adjacent sides being perpendicular), we will follow these steps:

### Step 1: Identify the Pairs of Parallel Sides
First, let's calculate the slopes between each pair of consecutive points to identify parallel sides.

- **Between (-2, -1) and (4, 0):**
  \[
  \text{Slope} = \frac{0 - (-1)}{4 - (-2)} = \frac{1}{6}
  \]

- **Between (4, 0) and (3, 3):**
  \[
  \text{Slope} = \frac{3 - 0}{3 - 4} = \frac{3}{-1} = -3
  \]

- **Between (3, 3) and (-3, 2):**
  \[
  \text{Slope} = \frac{2 - 3}{-3 - 3} = \frac{-1}{-6} = \frac{1}{6}
  \]

- **Between (-3, 2) and (-2, -1):**
  \[
  \text{Slope} = \frac{-1 - 2}{-2 - (-3)} = \frac{-3}{1} = -3
  \]

From these calculations:
- The slopes of the sides connecting **(-2, -1)-(4,0)** and **(3,3)-(-3,2)** are both \( \frac{1}{6} \), indicating they are parallel.
- The slopes of the sides connecting **(4,0)-(3,3)** and **(-3,2)-(-2,-1)** are both \( -3 \), indicating they are also parallel.

This confirms that all four points form a trapezoid with two pairs of opposite sides being parallel.

### Step 2: Determine if It's an Isosceles Trapezoid
An isosceles trapezoid has non-parallel sides (legs) of equal length and base angles equal. Let's verify the lengths of the legs:

1. **Length between (-2, -1) and (3, 3):**
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \sqrt{(3 - (-2))^2 + (3 - (-1))^2} = \sqrt{5^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{25 + 16} = \sqrt{41} \approx 6.403
   \]

2. **Length between (4, 0) and (-3, 2):**
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \sqrt{(-3 - 4)^2 + (2 - 0)^2} = \sqrt{(-7)^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{49 + 4} = \sqrt{53} \approx 7.280
   \]

Since the lengths of the legs are not equal, this trapezoid is **not** isosceles.

### Step 3: Check for Perpendicular Adjacent Sides
For any sides to be perpendicular, their slopes must be negative reciprocals (i.e., \( m_1 = -\frac{1}{m_2} \)).

- The first pair of parallel sides has a slope of \( \frac{1}{6} \). A perpendicular slope would be \( -6 \), which is not present among the other slopes.
- The second pair of parallel sides has a slope of \( -3 \). A perpendicular slope would be \( \frac{1}{3} \), which also isn't found in the remaining slopes.

Thus, **no adjacent sides are perpendicular**.

### Final Answer

The given points form an isosceles trapezoid that is not rectangular.

slope = (0 - 1) / (6 - 5)  
slope = (-1) / 1  
slope = -1

With the slope determined, I can now find the angle of inclination using the arctangent function:

θ = arctan(slope)  
θ = arctan(-1)

The arctangent of -1 corresponds to an angle of -45 degrees. However, angles are typically represented as positive values between 0 and 180 degrees in geometric contexts.

To find the equivalent positive angle:
θ = 180° + (-45°)  
θ = 135°

Therefore, the angle that the line makes with the positive x-axis is 135 degrees.

To determine the angle of inclination of the line passing through the points \((5, 1)\) and \((6, 0)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope
The slope \(m\) of a line through two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]
Substituting the given points:
\[
m = \frac{0 - 1}{6 - 5} = \frac{-1}{1} = -1
\]

### Step 2: Find the Angle of Inclination
The angle of inclination \(\theta\) is calculated using the arctangent function:
\[
\theta = \arctan(m)
\]
Substituting \(m = -1\):
\[
\theta = \arctan(-1) = -45^\circ
\]
Since angles are typically measured between \(0^\circ\) and \(180^\circ\), we convert the negative angle to its positive equivalent:
\[
\theta = 180^\circ + (-45^\circ) = 135^\circ
\]

\[

\]

Given \( \tan \theta = \frac{1}{3} \):

\[
\left| \frac{m}{1 + 2m^2} \right| = \frac{1}{3}

Squaring both sides:

\[

\]

- If one slope is \( 1 \), the other is \( 0.5 \).
- If one slope is \( -1 \), the other is \( -0.5 \).

To find the equation of the line passing through the points \((3, 1)\) and \((5, 7)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope (m)
The slope \(m\) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]
Substituting the given points:
\[
m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
\]

### Step 2: Use the Point-Slope Form
The point-slope form of a linear equation is:
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]
Using the point \((3, 1)\):
\[
y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
\]

### Step 3: Simplify to Slope-Intercept Form
Simplify the equation to get it in the form \(y = mx + b\):
\[
y - 1 = 3x - 9 \\
y = 3x - 8
\]

### Final Answer
The equation of the line passing through the points \((3, 1)\) and \((5, 7)\) is:
\[
\boxed{y = 3x - 8}
\]

Next, I calculate the slope (m) using these two points. The formula for slope is m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1). Plugging in the values gives me m = (4 - 0)/(0 - (-1)) = 4/1 = 4.

With the slope known, I use the intercept-intercept form of a line's equation: x/a + y/b = 1, where a is the x-intercept and b is the y-intercept. Substituting a = -1 and b = 4 into this formula results in x/(-1) + y/4 = 1.

To find the equation of a line that intersects the x-axis at \(-1\) and the y-axis at \(4\), follow these steps:

1. **Identify the intercepts**:
   - X-intercept (\(a\)): \((-1, 0)\)
   - Y-intercept (\(b\)): \((0, 4)\)

2. **Use the intercept form of the equation of a line**:
   
   The general formula is:
   \[
   \frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
   \]
   
3. **Substitute the values of \(a\) and \(b\)**:
   
   Plugging in \(a = -1\) and \(b = 4\):
   \[
   \frac{x}{-1} + \frac{y}{4} = 1
   \]
   
4. **Simplify the equation**:
   
   Multiply both sides by \(-4\) to eliminate the denominators:
   \[
   -4x + y = -4
   \]
   
5. **Rearrange the equation for clarity (optional)**:
   
   You can write it as:
   \[
   y = 4x - 4
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{y = 4x - 4}
\]

First, I need to determine if three points are collinear. To do this, I'll use the concept that if any two vectors formed by these points have a cross product of zero, then the points lie on the same straight line.

I'll label the points as A(3, 0), B(-2, -2), and C(8, 2).

Next, I'll calculate two vectors: vector AB and vector AC. 

For vector AB, subtracting the coordinates of point A from point B gives me (-5, -2). 

For vector AC, subtracting the coordinates of point A from point C results in (5, 2).

Now, I'll compute the cross product of these two vectors. The cross product for vectors (a, b) and (c, d) is calculated as (ad - bc). Applying this formula:

(-5)(2) - (-2)(5) = -10 + 10 = 0.

Since the cross product equals zero, it confirms that vectors AB and AC are parallel. Therefore, points A, B, and C are collinear.
</think>

To determine if the three points \((3, 0)\), \((-2, -2)\), and \((8, 2)\) are collinear, we can use the concept of vectors and cross product.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Label the Points:**
   - Point \(A(3, 0)\)
   - Point \(B(-2, -2)\)
   - Point \(C(8, 2)\)

2. **Calculate Vectors AB and AC:**
   - Vector \(\vec{AB}\) is calculated as:
     \[
     \vec{AB} = B - A = (-2 - 3, -2 - 0) = (-5, -2)
     \]
   - Vector \(\vec{AC}\) is calculated as:
     \[
     \vec{AC} = C - A = (8 - 3, 2 - 0) = (5, 2)
     \]

3. **Compute the Cross Product of Vectors AB and AC:**
   - The cross product in two dimensions simplifies to:
     \[
     \text{Cross Product} = (\vec{AB}_x \times \vec{AC}_y) - (\vec{AB}_y \times \vec{AC}_x)
     \]
   - Substituting the values:
     \[
     (-5) \times 2 - (-2) \times 5 = -10 + 10 = 0
     \]

4. **Determine Collinearity:**
   - If the cross product is zero, the vectors are parallel.
   - Therefore, points \(A\), \(B\), and \(C\) lie on a straight line.

**Final Answer:**

The problem involves determining the quantity of a product sold when the price increases from ₹116 to ₹117 per liter. We are given two data points: (p = 114, q = 1080) and (p = 116, q = 1320). 

To find the relationship between price (p) and quantity (q), we calculate the slope of the linear demand curve:

\[
\text{Slope} = \frac{\Delta q}{\Delta p} = \frac{1320 - 1080}{116 - 114} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
\]

This means for every increase of 1 paisa in price, the quantity sold increases by 120 liters. 

Using this slope, we can determine the quantity sold at p = 117:

\[
q = q \text{ at } p = 116 + (\Delta p) \times \text{slope} = 1320 + (117 - 116) \times 120 = 1320 + 120 = 1440
\]

Thus, the number of liters sold when the price is ₹117 per liter is:

\[
\boxed{1440}
\]

</think>

Given a line with slope \( m \) and y-intercept \( c \), we know that the x-intercept is at \( (a, 0) \) and the y-intercept is at \( (0, b) \). The equation of the line can be written in slope-intercept form as \( y = mx + c \). 

From the intercepts, we have:
- When \( x = a \), \( y = 0 \): \( 0 = ma + c \) which implies \( c = -ma \).
- The y-intercept \( b \) is equal to \( c \).

Thus, we have \( b = -ma \). 

The line also passes through the point \( (4, 5) \), so substituting this into the equation of the line gives:
\[ 5 = 4m + c \]
Since \( c = b \), we substitute to get:
\[ 5 = 4m + b \]

We are given that the sum of the intercepts is 13, i.e., \( a + b = 13 \). 

Substituting \( a = 13 - b \) into the equation for the point \( (4, 5) \):
\[ 5 = 4m + b \]
But since \( m = -\frac{b}{a} \), we substitute \( m \):
\[ 5 = 4\left(-\frac{b}{13 - b}\right) + b \]

Simplifying this equation:
\[ 5 = -\frac{4b}{13 - b} + b \]
Multiplying both sides by \( 13 - b \):
\[ 5(13 - b) = -4b + b(13 - b) \]
Expanding and simplifying:
\[ 65 - 5b = 9b - b^2 \]
Rearranging terms:
\[ b^2 - 14b + 65 = 0 \]

The discriminant of this quadratic equation is:
\[ (-14)^2 - 4 \cdot 1 \cdot 65 = 196 - 260 = -64 \]
Since the discriminant is negative, there are no real solutions for \( b \).

Thus, there is no real slope \( m \) that satisfies the given conditions.

\[
\boxed{\text{No such real slope exists}}
\]

\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{3 - 0}{2 - 1} = 3
\]
Using the point-slope form with point (1, 0):
\[
y - 0 = 3(x - 1) \\
y = 3x - 3
\]

First, I need to determine the slope of the line that passes through the points (–5, 7) and (4, 8). 

To calculate the slope \( m \), I use the formula:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]
Substituting the given points:
\[
m = \frac{8 - 7}{4 - (-5)} = \frac{1}{9}
\]

With the slope known, I can use the point-slope form of the equation to find \( b \). Let's use the point (–5, 7):
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]
Substituting:
\[
y - 7 = \frac{1}{9}(x + 5)
\]
Simplifying further:
\[
y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{5}{9} + 7
\]
To combine the constants, I convert 7 to ninths:
\[
y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{68}{9}
\]

Thus, the equation of the line in slope-intercept form is \( y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{68}{9} \).

To determine the equation of the line passing through the points \((-5, 7)\) and \((4, 8)\), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Slope (\(m\))**:
   
   The formula for slope between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   
   Substituting the given points:
   \[
   m = \frac{8 - 7}{4 - (-5)} = \frac{1}{9}
   \]

2. **Use Point-Slope Form to Find \(b\)**:
   
   The point-slope form is:
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   
   Using the point \((-5, 7)\):
   \[
   y - 7 = \frac{1}{9}(x + 5)
   \]
   
   Simplifying to find \(b\):
   \[
   y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{5}{9} + 7
   \]
   \[
   y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{68}{9}
   \]

Thus, the equation of the line in slope-intercept form is:

\[
y = \frac{1}{9}x + \frac{68}{9}
\]

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{\ y = \dfrac{1}{9}\ x + \dfrac{68}{9}}

First, let me recall what a median in a triangle is. A median connects a vertex to the midpoint of the opposing side. So for triangle PQR, there will be three medians: one from P to the midpoint of QR, one from Q to the midpoint of PR, and one from R to the midpoint of PQ.

I need to find the equations of these three lines. To do that, I'll first calculate the midpoints of each side because those are the points where the medians will connect to.

Let's start with finding the midpoint of QR. Q is at (-2,3) and R is at (4,5). The formula for the midpoint between two points (x₁,y₁) and (x₂,y₂) is ((x₁+x₂)/2, (y₁+y₂)/2).

Calculating the midpoint of QR:
Midpoint M1 = [(-2 + 4)/2, (3 + 5)/2] = (2/2, 8/2) = (1,4). So that's point M1(1,4).

Next, I'll find the midpoint of PR. P is at (2,1) and R is at (4,5).
Midpoint M2 = [(2+4)/2, (1+5)/2] = (6/2, 6/2) = (3,3). So M2(3,3).

Then, the midpoint of PQ. P is at (2,1) and Q is at (-2,3).
Midpoint M3 = [(2 + (-2))/2, (1+3)/2] = (0/2, 4/2) = (0,2). So M3(0,2).

First median: From P(2,1) to M1(1,4).

y = -3x + 7.

So the equation for this median is y = -3x + 7.

So since it passes through Q(-2,3), the equation is y = 3.

Third median: From R(4,5) to M3(0,2).
Calculating the slope m3:
m3 = (2 - 5)/(0 - 4) = (-3)/(-4) = 3/4.

Using point R(4,5):
y - 5 = (3/4)(x - 4)

4(y -5) = 3(x-4)

Bringing all terms to one side:
-3x +4y -8=0 or rearranged as y = (3/4)x + 2.

**Solution:**

We need to find the distance from the point \((5, -7)\) to the line \(5x - 6y - 28 = 0\).

The formula for the distance \(d\) from a point \((x_1, y_1)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is:

\[
d = \frac{|A x_1 + B y_1 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Given:
- Point: \((5, -7)\)
- Line: \(5x - 6y - 28 = 0\) ⇒ \(A = 5\), \(B = -6\), \(C = -28\)

Substitute the values into the formula:

\[
d = \frac{|5(5) + (-6)(-7) - 28|}{\sqrt{5^2 + (-6)^2}}
\]

Calculate numerator:
\[
|25 + 42 - 28| = |39| = 39
\]

Calculate denominator:
\[
\sqrt{25 + 36} = \sqrt{61}
\]

Therefore, the distance is:
\[
d = \frac{39}{\sqrt{61}}
\]

To find the distance between these two parallel lines, we use the formula for the distance between two parallel lines \(ax + by + c_1 = 0\) and \(ax + by + c_2 = 0\), which is:

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|c_1 - c_2|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}}

The equation of line BC can be found using points B(6,1) and C(3,4). The slope \( m \) is calculated as:

\[
m = \frac{4 - 1}{3 - 6} = \frac{3}{-3} = -1
\]

Using the point-slope form with point B(6,1):

\[
y - 1 = -1(x - 6) \\
y = -x + 7 \\
\text{Equation of line BC: } x + y - 7 = 0
\]

\[
d = \frac{|1*4 + 1*5 - 7|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2}} \\
= \frac{|4 + 5 - 7|}{\sqrt{2}} \\
= \frac{2}{\sqrt{2}} \\
= \sqrt{2}
\]

The equation of the perpendicular bisector for the points (5,6) and (1,4) can be found using these steps:

1. **Find the midpoint**:
   The midpoint \( M \) between two points \( (x_1, y_1) \) and \( (x_2, y_2) \) is calculated as:
   \[
   M = \left( \frac{x_1 + x_2}{2}, \frac{y_1 + y_2}{2} \right)
   \]
   For points (5,6) and (1,4):
   \[
   M = \left( \frac{5+1}{2}, \frac{6+4}{2} \right) = (3, 5)
   \]

2. **Calculate the slope of the line segment**:
   The slope \( m \) between two points is given by:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   Substituting the coordinates:
   \[
   m = \frac{4 - 6}{1 - 5} = \frac{-2}{-4} = 0.5
   \]

3. **Determine the slope of the perpendicular bisector**:
   The slope of a line perpendicular to another is the negative reciprocal of the original slope:
   \[
   m_{\text{perpendicular}} = -\frac{1}{m} = -\frac{1}{0.5} = -2
   \]

4. **Use point-slope form to find the equation**:
   The point-slope formula is:
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   Substituting \( m = -2 \) and midpoint (3,5):
   \[
   y - 5 = -2(x - 3)
   \]
   
5. **Simplify to slope-intercept form**:
   \[
   y = -2x + 6 + 5 \\
   y = -2x + 11
   \]

The equation of the perpendicular bisector is:

Now that I have both slopes, I can use the formula to calculate the angle between two lines:

tanθ = |(m₂ - m₁)/(1 + m₁*m₂)|

Substituting the values of m₁ and m₂ into this formula will allow me to find tanθ. Once I have tanθ, taking its arctangent will give me the angle θ in radians.

Finally, I'll convert the angle from radians to degrees for a more intuitive result.
</think>

To find the angle between the two lines given by the equations:

1. **First Line:** \( \sqrt{3}x + y = 1 \)
2. **Second Line:** \( x + \sqrt{3}y = 1 \)

### Step-by-Step Solution:

#### Step 1: Find the Slopes of Both Lines

**For the First Line (\( \sqrt{3}x + y = 1 \)):**
\[
y = -\sqrt{3}x + 1
\]
So, the slope \( m_1 = -\sqrt{3} \).

**For the Second Line (\( x + \sqrt{3}y = 1 \)):**
\[
\sqrt{3}y = -x + 1 \\
y = -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}x + \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
\]
So, the slope \( m_2 = -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \).

#### Step 2: Use the Formula for the Angle Between Two Lines

The formula to find the angle \( \theta \) between two lines with slopes \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \) is:
\[
\tan(\theta) = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2} \right|
\]

Substituting the values of \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \):
\[
\tan(\theta) = \left| \frac{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} - (-\sqrt{3})}{1 + (-\sqrt{3}) \times \left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right)} \right| \\
= \left| \frac{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} + \sqrt{3}}{1 + 1} \right| \\
= \left| \frac{\frac{-1 + 3}{\sqrt{3}}}{2} \right| \\
= \left| \frac{2/\sqrt{3}}{2} \right| \\
= \left| \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \right|
\]

#### Step 3: Find the Angle \( \theta \)

\[
\tan(\theta) = \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
\]
Taking the arctangent of both sides:
\[
\theta = \arctan\left( \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \right)
\]
We know that:
\[
\arctan\left( \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \right) = 30^\circ
\]

### Final Answer:

The angle between the two lines is \( 30^\circ \).

To find the distance between two parallel lines given by the equations:

1. \( 19x + 12y - 38 = 0 \)
2. \( 38x + 24y + 70 = 0 \)

**Step-by-Step Solution:**

1. **Verify Parallelism:**
   - The first line can be written as \( y = (-\frac{19}{12})x + \frac{38}{12} \), so its slope is \( -\frac{19}{12} \).
   - Dividing the second equation by 2, we get \( 19x + 12y + 35 = 0 \), which also has a slope of \( -\frac{19}{12} \).
   - Since both lines have the same slope, they are parallel.

2. **Apply the Distance Formula for Parallel Lines:**
   
   The distance (\( d \)) between two parallel lines \( ax + by + c_1 = 0 \) and \( ax + by + c_2 = 0 \) is given by:
   \[
   d = \frac{|c_2 - c_1|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}}
   \]
   
3. **Identify Coefficients:**
   - For the first line \( 19x + 12y - 38 = 0 \), \( a = 19 \), \( b = 12 \), and \( c_1 = -38 \).
   - For the second line, after dividing by 2 to match coefficients with the first line's multiples:
     \[
     19x + 12y + 35 = 0
     \]
     Here, \( a = 19 \), \( b = 12 \), and \( c_2 = 35 \).

4. **Calculate the Distance:**
   \[
   d = \frac{|35 - (-38)|}{\sqrt{19^2 + 12^2}} = \frac{73}{\sqrt{361 + 144}} = \frac{73}{\sqrt{505}}
   \]
   
5. **Simplify the Expression (if necessary):**
   - Rationalizing the denominator:
     \[
     d = \frac{73}{\sqrt{505}} \times \frac{\sqrt{505}}{\sqrt{505}} = \frac{73\sqrt{505}}{505}
     \]
   - However, the simplest form is \( \frac{73}{\sqrt{505}} \).

**Final Answer:**

To find the **distance of a point** from a line using its **intercept form**, follow these steps:

### Given:
The equation of the line is **12x - 9y = 18**.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Rewrite the Equation in Slope-Intercept Form:**
   
   Start by expressing \( y \) in terms of \( x \):
   
   \[
   12x - 9y = 18
   \]
   
   Subtract \( 12x \) from both sides:
   
   \[
   -9y = -12x + 18
   \]
   
   Divide all terms by \( -3 \):
   
   \[
   y = \frac{4}{3}x - 2
   \]

2. **Identify the Coefficients:**
   
   The equation is now in slope-intercept form \( y = mx + b \), where:
   
   \[
   m = \frac{4}{3},\quad b = -2
   \]
   
   For standard form, rearrange the original simplified equation to:
   
   \[
   4x - 3y = 6
   \]
   
   So, \( A = 4 \), \( B = -3 \), and \( C = -2 \).

3. **Apply the Distance Formula:**
   
   The formula for the distance from a point \( (x_0, y_0) \) to the line \( Ax + By + C = 0 \) is:
   
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{|Ax_0 + By_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
   \]
   
4. **Calculate the Distance:**
   
   Plug in \( A = 4 \), \( B = -3 \), and \( C = -2 \):
   
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{|4x_0 - 3y_0 - 2|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-3)^2}}
   \]
   
5. **Simplify the Expression:**
   
   Compute the denominator:
   
   \[
   \sqrt{16 + 9} = \sqrt{25} = 5
   \]
   
   Therefore, the distance simplifies to:
   
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{|4x_0 - 3y_0 - 2|}{5}
   \]

### Final Answer:

\boxed{\dfrac{|4x_0 - 3y_0 - 2|}{5}}

The distance from the point (1, 2) to the line defined by \(4x + 7y + 5 = 0\) is calculated using the formula for the distance from a point to a line. 

Using the formula:
\[
d = \frac{|Ax_1 + By_1 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]
where \(A = 4\), \(B = 7\), and \(C = 5\). Substituting the point (1, 2) into the formula:
\[
d = \frac{|4(1) + 7(2) + 5|}{\sqrt{4^2 + 7^2}} = \frac{|4 + 14 + 5|}{\sqrt{16 + 49}} = \frac{23}{\sqrt{65}}
\]
Rationalizing the denominator:
\[
d = \frac{23\sqrt{65}}{65}
\]

The final answer is \(\boxed{\frac{23\sqrt{65}}{65}}\).

To find the distance between two parallel lines given by their equations, follow these steps:

**Given Lines:**
\[
9x + 6y - 7 = 0
\]
\[
3x + 2y + 6 = 0
\]

**Step 1: Ensure Both Lines Are in the Same Format**

First, make sure both equations are written in a comparable form. We'll adjust the second equation to match the coefficients of \(x\) and \(y\) from the first equation.

Multiply the entire second equation by 3:
\[
3(3x + 2y) = 3(-6)
\]
\[
9x + 6y - 18 = 0
\]

Now, both equations are:
\[
9x + 6y - 7 = 0 \quad \text{(Equation 1)}
\]
\[
9x + 6y - 18 = 0 \quad \text{(Equation 2)}
\]

**Step 2: Identify the Coefficients**

From Equation 1:
\[
A_1 = 9,\ B_1 = 6,\ C_1 = -7
\]

From Equation 2:
\[
A_2 = 9,\ B_2 = 6,\ C_2 = -18
\]

**Step 3: Apply the Distance Formula**

The distance (\(d\)) between two parallel lines \(Ax + By + C = 0\) and \(Ax + By + D = 0\) is given by:
\[
d = \frac{|C_1 - C_2|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Substitute the values from Equations 1 and 2:
\[
d = \frac{|-7 - (-18)|}{\sqrt{9^2 + 6^2}} = \frac{|11|}{\sqrt{81 + 36}} = \frac{11}{\sqrt{117}} = \frac{11}{3\sqrt{13}}
\]

**Step 4: Simplify the Expression (if necessary)**

To rationalize the denominator:
\[
d = \frac{11}{3\sqrt{13}} \times \frac{\sqrt{13}}{\sqrt{13}} = \frac{11\sqrt{13}}{39}
\]

However, for simplicity, we often leave it as:
\[
d = \frac{11}{3\sqrt{13}}
\]

**Final Answer:**

The distance between the two parallel lines is:
\[
\boxed{\dfrac{25}{3\sqrt{13}}}
\]

To find the **slope** (\( m \)) of the line passing through the points \( (6, -4) \) and \( (-2, 8) \), we can use the formula for the slope between two points:

\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

**Step-by-Step Solution:**

1. **Identify the coordinates:**
   - First point (\( (x_1, y_1) \)): \( (6, -4) \)
   - Second point (\( (x_2, y_2) \)): \( (-2, 8) \)

2. **Substitute the values into the slope formula:**
   \[

To find the value of \( x \) for the point \((x, 26)\) lying on the line passing through the points \((0, 8)\) and \((6, 10)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope (\( m \)) of the Line

The slope \( m \) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Plugging in the values:
\[
m = \frac{10 - 8}{6 - 0} = \frac{2}{6} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

### Step 2: Determine the Equation of the Line

Using the slope-intercept form \( y = mx + b \), where \( m \) is the slope and \( b \) is the y-intercept:

We can use one of the given points to find \( b \). Let's use the point \((0, 8)\):
\[
8 = \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)(0) + b \implies b = 8
\]

So, the equation of the line is:
\[
y = \frac{1}{3}x + 8
\]

### Step 3: Find \( x \) When \( y = 26 \)

Set \( y = 26 \) in the equation and solve for \( x \):
\[
26 = \frac{1}{3}x + 8
\]
Subtract 8 from both sides:
\[
18 = \frac{1}{3}x
\]
Multiply both sides by 3 to solve for \( x \):
\[
x = 54
\]

### Final Answer

The value of \( x \) is:
\[
\boxed{x=54}
\]

To find a point on the \( x \)-axis equidistant from the points \((9, 8)\) and \((5, 6)\), we can follow these steps:

1. **Determine the Midpoint**:
   The midpoint between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
   \[
   \left( \frac{x_1 + x_2}{2}, \frac{y_1 + y_2}{2} \right)
   \]
   Substituting the given points:
   \[
   \left( \frac{9 + 5}{2}, \frac{8 + 6}{2} \right) = (7, 7)
   \]

2. **Find the Slope of the Line Segment**:
   The slope \( m \) between two points is calculated as:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   Substituting the given points:
   \[
   m = \frac{6 - 8}{5 - 9} = \frac{-2}{-4} = \frac{1}{2}
   \]

3. **Determine the Perpendicular Bisector**:
   The perpendicular bisector of a line segment has a slope that is the negative reciprocal of the original slope. Therefore, the slope of the perpendicular bisector is \( -2 \).

   Using the midpoint \((7, 7)\) and the new slope in the point-slope form equation:
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   Substituting the values:
   \[
   y - 7 = -2(x - 7)
   \]

4. **Find the Intersection with the \( x \)-axis**:
   On the \( x \)-axis, \( y = 0 \). Substitute \( y = 0 \) into the equation of the perpendicular bisector to find \( x \):
   \[
   0 - 7 = -2(x - 7)
   \]
   Simplifying:
   \[
   -7 = -2x + 14
   \]
   \[
   -2x = -21
   \]
   \[
   x = \frac{21}{2} = 10.5
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{10.5}

First, I identify the four points given: (-2, -1), (4, 0), (3, 3), and (-3, 2). To determine if these points form a parallelogram that is also a rectangle, I need to check two main properties.

I start by calculating the slopes of all sides. The slope formula is m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1).

Calculating Slopes:
- Between (-2, -1) and (4, 0): 
Slope = (0 - (-1))/(4 - (-2)) = 1/6
- Between (4, 0) and (3, 3):
Slope = (3 - 0)/(3 - 4) = 3/-1 = -3
- Between (3, 3) and (-3, 2):
Slope = (2 - 3)/(-3 - 3) = -1/-6 = 1/6
- Between (-3, 2) and (-2, -1):
Slope = (-1 - 2)/(-2 - (-3)) = -3/1 = -3

Next, I compare the slopes to verify opposite sides are equal:
- Slopes of 1/6 (between first and third points) match.
- Slopes of -3 (between second and fourth points) also match.

Then, I check adjacent sides for perpendicularity by multiplying their slopes. For a rectangle, adjacent sides should be negative reciprocals (their product should be -1).
- Slope 1/6 multiplied by slope -3 equals -1, confirming perpendicularity.

To determine if the given points \((-2, -1)\), \((4, 0)\), \((3, 3)\), and \((-3, 2)\) form a rectangle (a special type of parallelogram with right angles), we'll follow these steps:

### Step 1: Calculate Slopes Between Consecutive Points

We'll calculate the slopes between each pair of consecutive points using the slope formula:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

1. **Between \((-2, -1)\) and \((4, 0)\):**
   \[
   m = \frac{0 - (-1)}{4 - (-2)} = \frac{1}{6}
   \]
   
2. **Between \((4, 0)\) and \((3, 3)\):**
   \[
   m = \frac{3 - 0}{3 - 4} = \frac{3}{-1} = -3
   \]
   
3. **Between \((3, 3)\) and \((-3, 2)\):**
   \[
   m = \frac{2 - 3}{-3 - 3} = \frac{-1}{-6} = \frac{1}{6}
   \]
   
4. **Between \((-3, 2)\) and \((-2, -1)\):**
   \[
   m = \frac{-1 - 2}{-2 - (-3)} = \frac{-3}{1} = -3
   \]

### Step 2: Verify Opposite Sides Have Equal Slopes

In a parallelogram, opposite sides are parallel and thus have equal slopes.

- **Slope of first side:** \(\frac{1}{6}\)
- **Slope of third side (opposite):** \(\frac{1}{6}\)

- **Slope of second side:** \(-3\)
- **Slope of fourth side (opposite):** \(-3\)

This confirms that opposite sides are equal in slope, indicating a parallelogram.

### Step 3: Check Adjacent Sides for Perpendicularity

In a rectangle, adjacent sides must be perpendicular, meaning their slopes' product equals \(-1\).

- **Product of first and second slopes:**
  \[
  \frac{1}{6} \times (-3) = -\frac{3}{6} = -0.5
  \]
  
Since this does not equal \(-1\), the adjacent sides are not perpendicular.

### Conclusion

The points form a parallelogram but do not satisfy the condition for being a rectangle (right angles). Therefore, they do **not** form a rectangle.

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{\text{No}}

**Solution:**

Let's denote the slopes of the two lines as \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \).

According to the problem:
\[ m_1 = \frac{2}{3} m_2 \]

The tangent of the angle (\( \tan\theta \)) between two lines with slopes \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \) is given by:
\[ \tan\theta = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2} \right| \]

Given that \( \tan\theta = \frac{1}{3} \), we substitute the expressions into the formula:

\[ \frac{1}{3} = \left| \frac{m_2 - \frac{2}{3}m_2}{1 + \left( \frac{2}{3}m_2 \right)m_2} \right| \]

Simplify the numerator:
\[ m_2 - \frac{2}{3}m_2 = \frac{1}{3}m_2 \]

So,
\[ \frac{1}{3} = \left| \frac{\frac{1}{3}m_2}{1 + \frac{2}{3}m_2^2} \right| \]

Multiply both sides by the denominator:
\[ \frac{1}{3} \left( 1 + \frac{2}{3}m_2^2 \right) = \frac{1}{3}m_2 \]

Simplify further:
\[ \frac{1}{3} + \frac{2}{9}m_2^2 = \frac{1}{3}m_2 \]

Multiply all terms by 9 to eliminate denominators:
\[ 3 + 2m_2^2 = 3m_2 \]

Rearrange the equation into standard quadratic form:
\[ 2m_2^2 - 3m_2 + 3 = 0 \]

Solve this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula:
\[ m_2 = \frac{3 \pm \sqrt{(-3)^2 - 4 \cdot 2 \cdot 3}}{2 \cdot 2} = \frac{3 \pm \sqrt{9 - 24}}{4} = \frac{3 \pm \sqrt{-15}}{4} \]

Since the discriminant is negative, there are no real solutions for \( m_2 \).

However, if we consider complex slopes (which isn't typical in most practical problems), then:
\[ m_2 = \frac{3 \pm i\sqrt{15}}{4} \]

Therefore, the final answer is:

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{\text{No real solution exists for the given conditions.}}

m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)
= (4 - 0)/(0 - (-1))
= 4/1
= 4

Now that I have the slope, I can use the intercept-intercept form of a line equation:
x/a + y/b = 1
where a is the x-intercept and b is the y-intercept.

Substituting a = -1 and b = 4:

x/(-1) + y/4 = 1

Simplifying, I get:

-x + (y)/4 = 1

To express this in slope-intercept form, I'll rearrange it to solve for y:

(y)/4 = x + 1
Multiply both sides by 4:
y = 4x + 4

Therefore, the equation of the line is y = 4x + 4.
</think>

To find the **equation of a line** that intersects the **x-axis at -1** and the **y-axis at 4**, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Identify the Intercepts
- **X-intercept (a):** \(-1\)
- **Y-intercept (b):** \(4\)

### Step 2: Use the Intercept-Intercept Form of a Line
The general equation of a line using intercepts is:
\[
\frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
\]

Substituting the given values:
\[
\frac{x}{-1} + \frac{y}{4} = 1
\]
Simplify the equation:
\[
-x + \frac{y}{4} = 1
\]

### Step 3: Convert to Slope-Intercept Form (Optional)
To express the equation in the form \(y = mx + c\), rearrange the terms:
\[
\frac{y}{4} = x + 1
\]
Multiply both sides by 4:
\[
y = 4x + 4
\]

### Final Answer
The **equation of the line** is:
\[
\boxed{y = 4x + 4}
\]

First, I need to determine if three given points are collinear. The points provided are (3,0), (-2,-2), and (8,2).

To check for collinearity, I can use the slope formula between pairs of points. If all slopes between any two pairs are equal, the points lie on the same straight line.

First, calculate the slope between (3,0) and (-2,-2):
Slope = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1) = (-2 - 0)/(-2 - 3) = (-2)/(-5) = 2/5

Next, calculate the slope between (-2,-2) and (8,2):
Slope = (2 - (-2))/(8 - (-2)) = 4/10 = 2/5

Since both slopes are equal to 2/5, all three points lie on the same straight line. Therefore, they are collinear.
</think>

To determine if the three points \((3, 0)\), \((-2, -2)\), and \((8, 2)\) are collinear (lying on the same straight line), we can use the concept of slopes between pairs of points.

### Step-by-Step Explanation:

1. **Calculate the Slope Between Two Pairs of Points:**
   
   The slope \(m\) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   
   - **Slope Between \((3, 0)\) and \((-2, -2)\):**
     \[
     m_1 = \frac{-2 - 0}{-2 - 3} = \frac{-2}{-5} = \frac{2}{5}
     \]
   
   - **Slope Between \((-2, -2)\) and \((8, 2)\):**
     \[
     m_2 = \frac{2 - (-2)}{8 - (-2)} = \frac{4}{10} = \frac{2}{5}
     \]
   
   - **Slope Between \((3, 0)\) and \((8, 2)\):**
     \[
     m_3 = \frac{2 - 0}{8 - 3} = \frac{2}{5}
     \]

2. **Analyze the Slopes:**
   
   Since all three slopes \(m_1\), \(m_2\), and \(m_3\) are equal (\(m_1 = m_2 = m_3 = \frac{2}{5}\)), this indicates that all three points lie on the same straight line.

### Final Answer:
The three points are collinear.

**Final Answer**
\boxed{(d - b)x + (a - c)y = da - cb}
</think>

Given points \( A(a, b) \) and \( B(c, d) \), we need to show that the equation of the line passing through these points is given by:

\[
(d - b)x + (a - c)y = da - cb
\]

We can derive this using multiple methods such as parametric equations, determinant expansion, or vector approach. Here, we use the determinant method.

The line passing through points \( A(a, b) \) and \( B(c, d) \) can be represented by the determinant:

\[
\begin{vmatrix}
x & y & 1 \\
a & b & 1 \\
c & d & 1
\end{vmatrix} = 0
\]

Expanding this determinant, we get:

\[
x(b - d) + a(d - y) + c(y - b) = 0
\]

Simplifying the terms, we have:

\[
(d - b)x + (a - c)y = ad - bc
\]

Thus, the equation of the line is:

\[
(d - b)x + (a - c)y = da - cb
\]

Hence proved.

### Final Answer
\boxed{(d - b)x + (a - c)y = da - cb}

First, I need to find the equation of a line that makes an angle of \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) radians with the positive x-axis and passes through the point (0, 2).

The slope (\( m \)) of the line can be determined using the tangent of the given angle. So, 
\[ m = \tan\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right) \]
Since \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) is in the second quadrant where tangent values are negative,
\[ m = -\sqrt{3} \]

With the slope known and the line passing through (0, 2), which is the y-intercept (\( b \)), I can use the slope-intercept form of a linear equation:
\[ y = mx + b \]
Substituting the values gives:
\[ y = -\sqrt{3}x + 2 \]

Next, to find the x-intercept, set \( y = 0 \) in the equation:
\[ 0 = -\sqrt{3}x + 2 \]
Solving for \( x \),
\[ \sqrt{3}x = 2 \]
\[ x = \frac{2}{\sqrt{3}} \]
Rationalizing the denominator,
\[ x = \frac{2\sqrt{3}}{3} \]

Therefore, the line intersects the x-axis at \( \left( \frac{2\sqrt{3}}{3}, 0 \right) \).
</think>

To determine the equation of a line that makes an angle of \( \theta = \frac{2\pi}{3} \) radians with the positive x-axis and passes through the point (0, 2), follow these steps:

1. **Find the Slope (\( m \))**:
   The slope of the line is given by the tangent of the angle it makes with the x-axis.
   \[
   m = \tan\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right)
   \]
   Since \( \frac{2\pi}{3} \) radians corresponds to 120°, and the tangent of this angle is negative,
   \[
   m = -\sqrt{3}
   \]

2. **Determine the Y-Intercept (\( b \))**:
   The line passes through (0, 2), which is its y-intercept.
   \[
   b = 2
   \]

3. **Write the Equation of the Line**:
   Using the slope-intercept form \( y = mx + b \),
   \[
   y = -\sqrt{3}x + 2
   \]
   
4. **Find the X-Intercept**:
   To find where the line crosses the x-axis, set \( y = 0 \) and solve for \( x \):
   \[
   0 = -\sqrt{3}x + 2 \\
   \sqrt{3}x = 2 \\
   x = \frac{2}{\sqrt{3}} = \frac{2\sqrt{3}}{3}
   \]

5. **Equation of the Parallel Line**:
   Any line parallel to the given line will have the same slope (\( m = -\sqrt{3} \)). Assuming it passes through another point (for generality, let's assume it also passes through (0, 2)), its equation would be identical:
   \[
   y = -\sqrt{3}x + 2
   \]
   
6. **Y-Intercept of the Parallel Line**:
   Since both parallel lines have the same slope and pass through the same point (0, 2), their y-intercepts are also the same.
   \[
   b = 2
   \]

---

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\text{Equation of the given line}: \quad &y &= -\sqrt{3}x + 2 \\
&\text{X-intercept}: \quad &\left( \frac{2\sqrt{3}}{3}, 0 \right) \\
&\text{Equation of any parallel line}: \quad &y = -\sqrt{3}x + 2 \\
&\text{Y-intercept for all parallel lines}: \quad & (0, 2)
\end{aligned}
}
\]

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Problem Interpretation:** 
   - We are asked to find a straight line passing through the point (4, 4) such that the sum of its x-intercept and y-intercept is 13.

2. **Equation Setup:**
   - The general equation of a line in intercept form is \( \frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1 \), where \( a \) is the x-intercept and \( b \) is the y-intercept.
   - Given that the sum of intercepts is 13, we have \( a + b = 13 \).

3. **Substitution:**
   - Express one intercept in terms of the other using \( b = 13 - a \).
   - Substitute into the line equation with point (4,4):
     \[
     \frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{b} = 1
     \]
     Substituting \( b \) gives:
     \[
     \frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{13 - a} = 1
     \]

4. **Solving the Equation:**
   - Multiply both sides by \( a(13 - a) \):
     \[
     4(13 - a) + 4a = a(13 - a)
     \]
     Simplifying:
     \[
     52 - 4a + 4a = 13a - a^2
     \]
     Leading to:
     \[
     a^2 - 13a + 52 = 0
     \]

5. **Discriminant Check:**
   - Compute the discriminant \( D = (-13)^2 - 4(1)(52) = 169 - 208 = -39 \).
   - Since \( D < 0 \), there are no real solutions for \( a \).

**Conclusion:** There is no real straight line that passes through (4, 4) with x-intercept and y-intercept summing to 13.

Alright, let's tackle this problem step by step. So, we have two points: (1, 0) and (2, 3). We need to find the equation of the line that passes through these two points.

First, I remember that to find the equation of a straight line, one common method is using the slope-intercept form, which is y = mx + b. Here, m represents the slope or gradient of the line, and b is the y-intercept where the line crosses the y-axis.

To start, let's calculate the slope (m). The formula for the slope between two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Plugging in our points: (1, 0) as (x1, y1) and (2, 3) as (x2, y2).

So,
y2 - y1 = 3 - 0 = 3
x2 - x1 = 2 - 1 = 1

Therefore, m = 3 / 1 = 3.

Now that we have the slope, the next step is to find the y-intercept (b). We can use one of the given points in the equation y = mx + b. Let's choose point (1, 0) because it might simplify calculations.

Substituting x = 1 and y = 0 into the equation:

0 = 3(1) + b
0 = 3 + b

To solve for b, we subtract 3 from both sides:
b = -3

Now that we have both m and b, we can write the full equation of the line.

So plugging back into y = mx + b:

y = 3x - 3

Just to make sure everything is correct, I'll verify with the second point (2, 3).

Substitute x = 2:
y = 3(2) - 3
y = 6 - 3
y = 3

Which matches the given y-coordinate of the second point. So our calculations seem accurate.

Alternatively, if we want to present this in a different form, like standard form (Ax + By = C), we can rearrange the equation:

Starting from y = 3x - 3,

Subtract 3x from both sides:
-3x + y = -3

Multiply through by -1 to make it look neater:
3x - y = 3

So, that's another valid form of the line equation.

In conclusion, there are two forms we can present the equation:

1. Slope-intercept form: y = 3x - 3
2. Standard form: 3x - y = 3

Both accurately represent the line passing through the points (1, 0) and (2, 3).

To find the equation of a line that passes through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**

   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]

   \[

   \[

   \]
   
   Convert 7 to ninths:
   \[

   \]

I need to find the distance from the point (5, -7) to the line given by the equation 5x - 6y - 28 = 0. I remember that there's a formula for calculating this distance without having to draw any graphs or visualize in three dimensions.

First, let me write down the standard form of the line and identify the coefficients A, B, and C from the given equation. The general formula for the distance (d) from a point (x₀, y₀) to a line Ax + By + C = 0 is:

d = |A*x₀ + B*y₀ + C| / sqrt(A² + B²)

For the line equation provided:
5x -6y -28 = 0
Here, A is 5, B is -6, and C is -28.

Next, I'll plug in the coordinates of the point (5, -7) into the formula. Let's compute each part step by step to minimize errors.

Calculating the numerator:
|A*x₀ + B*y₀ + C|
= |5*5 + (-6)*(-7) + (-28)|
First, 5 multiplied by 5 is 25.
Then, -6 multiplied by -7 gives me 42.
Adding these together: 25 + 42 = 67
Now, subtracting 28 from 67: 67 - 28 = 39
Taking the absolute value of 39 is just 39.

Calculating the denominator:
sqrt(A² + B²)
= sqrt(5² + (-6)²)
First, square each coefficient: 5 squared is 25, and (-6) squared is 36.
Adding these together: 25 + 36 = 61
Taking the square root of 61 gives approximately 7.81.

Finally, dividing the numerator by the denominator:
Distance = 39 / sqrt(61)

To provide a more precise answer, I can rationalize or approximate this value further if needed.
</think>

The distance from the point (5, -7) to the line \(5x - 6y - 28 = 0\) is calculated using the formula:

\[
d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Substituting the values:
- \( A = 5 \), \( B = -6 \), \( C = -28 \)
- \( x_0 = 5 \), \( y_0 = -7 \)

\[
d = \frac{|5 \times 5 + (-6) \times (-7) - 28|}{\sqrt{5^2 + (-6)^2}} = \frac{39}{\sqrt{61}}
\]

The final answer is:

{"Final Answer": "39 / sqrt(61)"}

Alright, let's solve this step by step.

**Problem:** Find the equation of the tangent line to the parabola \( y^2 = 8x \) at the point (2,4).

**Solution:**

1. **Identify the given information:**
   - Parabola: \( y^2 = 8x \)
   - Point: (2, 4)

2. **Find the slope of the tangent line at the given point:**
   - Differentiate both sides with respect to x:
     \[
     2y \cdot \frac{dy}{dx} = 8
     \]
     Simplify:
     \[
     \frac{dy}{dx} = \frac{4}{y}
     \]
   - At point (2,4), \( y = 4 \):
     \[
     m = \frac{4}{4} = 1
     \]

3. **Use the point-slope form to find the equation of the tangent line:**
   - Point-Slope Formula:
     \[
     y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
     \]
   - Plug in \( m = 1 \), \( (x_1, y_1) = (2,4) \):
     \[
     y - 4 = 1(x - 2)
     \]
     Simplify:
     \[
     y = x + 2
     \]

**Answer:** The equation of the tangent line is \( y = x + 2 \). So, in terms of Y and C, it's \( Y = X + 2 \).

However, if we're considering a different scenario where another value for C was found (like -2), that would require reevaluating based on additional constraints or different curves. But given the standard calculation here, \( C = 2 \).

First, I identify the given point (1, 5) and the line equation \(3x - 4y - 16 = 0\).

Next, I use the distance formula to calculate the perpendicular distance from the point to the line.

The distance formula is \(\frac{|Ax + By + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}\), where A and B are coefficients of x and y in the line equation, and C is the constant term.

Plugging in the values: \(A = 3\), \(B = -4\), \(C = -16\), \(x = 1\), and \(y = 5\).

Calculating the numerator: \(|3(1) + (-4)(5) - 16| = |3 - 20 - 16| = |-33| = 33\).

Then, finding the denominator: \(\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16} = \sqrt{25} = 5\).

Finally, dividing the numerator by the denominator to get the distance: \(33 / 5 = 6.6\).

**Solution:**

To find the perpendicular distance from the point \((1, 5)\) to the line \(3x - 4y - 16 = 0\), we use the formula for the distance from a point \((x_1, y_1)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\):

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|A x_1 + B y_1 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

**Step-by-Step Calculation:**

1. **Identify the coefficients from the line equation \(3x - 4y - 16 = 0\):**
   \[
   A = 3,\quad B = -4,\quad C = -16
   \]
   
2. **Plug in the values into the distance formula:**
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{|3(1) + (-4)(5) - 16|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}}
   \]

3. **Calculate the numerator:**
   \[
   |3(1) + (-4)(5) - 16| = |3 - 20 - 16| = |-33| = 33
   \]
   
4. **Calculate the denominator:**
   \[
   \sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16} = \sqrt{25} = 5
   \]
   
5. **Divide the numerator by the denominator to get the distance:**
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{33}{5} = 6.6
   \]

Alright, so I have this problem where I need to find the angle between two lines given by their equations: √3 x + y = 1 and x + √3 y = 1. Hmm, okay, let's see how I can approach this.

First off, I remember that when you have two lines in a plane, they usually intersect at some point unless they're parallel or coinciding. Since these equations look different from each other, I think they should intersect somewhere. Now, the angle between them... I recall there's a formula involving their slopes. Let me try to find the slopes first.

For the first line, √3 x + y = 1, I can rearrange it into slope-intercept form (y = mx + b) where m is the slope. So subtracting √3 x from both sides gives y = -√3 x + 1. That means the slope (m₁) of the first line is -√3.

Now for the second line, x + √3 y = 1. Let me solve for y to get it into slope-intercept form. Subtracting x from both sides gives √3 y = -x + 1. Then dividing everything by √3, I get y = (-1/√3) x + (1/√3). So the slope (m₂) of the second line is -1/√3.

Okay, so now I have both slopes: m₁ = -√3 and m₂ = -1/√3. Now I need to find the angle between these two lines. The formula for the tangent of the angle θ between two lines with slopes m₁ and m₂ is tanθ = |(m₂ - m₁)/(1 + m₁*m₂)|.

Let me plug in the values:

tanθ = |(-1/√3 - (-√3)) / (1 + (-√3)*(-1/√3))|.

Simplify numerator: (-1/√3) + √3. Let's combine these terms by finding a common denominator, which would be √3.

   \[

The distance from the point (-2, 2) to the line defined by \(24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)\) is calculated using the standard distance formula. After converting the given equation into the standard form and applying the formula, the distance is found to be exactly 10 units.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Rewrite the Given Equation:**
   Start by expanding both sides of \(24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)\):
   \[
   24x + 288 = 10y - 40
   \]
   
2. **Convert to Standard Form:**
   Bring all terms to one side:
   \[
   24x - 10y + 328 = 0
   \]
   Here, \(A = 24\), \(B = -10\), and \(C = 328\).

3. **Apply the Distance Formula:**
   The distance \(d\) from point \((-2, 2)\) is:
   \[
   d = \frac{|24(-2) + (-10)(2) + 328|}{\sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2}}
   \]
   
4. **Calculate Numerator:**
   Compute each term:
   - \(24 \times (-2) = -48\)
   - \(-10 \times 2 = -20\)
   - Adding these with \(C\): \(-48 - 20 + 328 = 260\)
   
5. **Calculate Denominator:**
   Compute the square root:
   \[
   \sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2} = \sqrt{576 + 100} = \sqrt{676} = 26
   \]
   
6. **Compute Distance:**
   Divide numerator by denominator:
   \[
   d = \frac{260}{26} = 10
   \]

**Answer:**  
The distance is exactly 10 units.

The equation 12x - 9y = 18 can be rewritten in intercept form by dividing both sides by 18:

\[ \frac{12}{18}x - \frac{9}{18}y = 1 \]

Simplifying the fractions gives:

\[ \frac{2}{3}x - \frac{1}{2}y = 1 \]

This can be expressed in intercept form as:

\[ \frac{x}{\frac{3}{2}} + \frac{y}{-2} = 1 \]

Thus, the x-intercept is \( \left(\frac{3}{2}, 0\right) \) and the y-intercept is \( (0, -2) \).

The distance from point P(4, 1) to the line 4x - y = 0 can be found using the formula for the distance from a point to a line in coordinate geometry.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the coefficients of the line equation (Ax + By + C = 0):**
   - Given line: 4x - y = 0
   - A = 4, B = -1, C = 0

2. **Apply the distance formula:**
   - Distance \( d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \)
   - Plugging in the values:
     - \( A = 4, B = -1, C = 0 \)
     - Point P(x₀, y₀) = (4, 1)

3. **Calculate the numerator:**
   - \( |4*4 + (-1)*1 + 0| = |16 - 1| = |15| = 15 \)

4. **Calculate the denominator:**
   - \( \sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2} = \sqrt{16 + 1} = \sqrt{17} \)

5. **Final distance calculation:**
   - \( d = \frac{15}{\sqrt{17}} \)
   - Rationalizing the denominator:
     - Multiply numerator and denominator by \( \sqrt{17} \):
       - \( d = \frac{15 \sqrt{17}}{17} \)

**Final Answer:**
The distance from point P(4, 1) to the line 4x - y = 0 is \( \frac{15\sqrt{17}}{17} \).

To find the equation of a line parallel to both \(9x + 6y - 7 = 0\) and \(3x + 2y + 6 = 0\), we note that parallel lines have proportional coefficients. 

We can take the first given line, \(9x + 6y = 7\), and create another equation with the same coefficients but a different constant term. For instance, choosing \(14\) as the new constant gives:

**Answer:**
\( \boxed{9x + 6y = 14} \)
